# Part 2 — Utilities (src/utils)

## src/utils/ — Reference Documentation

This document covers every file in `src/utils/`. Files fall into two groups:

- **Category A** — custom, app-specific utility code, documented in full detail (every exported function/method, parameters, and meaningful state fields).
- **Category B** — vendored third-party library code (Three.js examples, CSG libraries). Documented at the level of purpose + public API surface actually consumed by the rest of the app, not line-by-line internals.

---

## src/utils/CropFabricImage.js

**Purpose.** `CropFabricImage` is a small self-contained "crop tool" class used to let a user draw/drag a crop rectangle over an image rendered onto a plain `<canvas>` element (not a Fabric.js canvas, despite the filename), then export the cropped region as a PNG data URL. It is used by the 3D preview/laser-preview screens (`src/components/3d-triple`, `src/components/3d-preview`, `src/components/3d-laser-preview`) to let a user crop a texture/photo before it is mapped onto a 3D model. It implements its own hit-testing, dragging of the four crop-rectangle corners, and both mouse and touch event handling (including basic mobile offset compensation).

### Exports (default export: class `CropFabricImage`)

Constructor: `new CropFabricImage(image, ImageLogoLink, nameCanvas, sizeCanvasWidth, sizeCanvasHeight, is_mobile)`
- `image` — URL/data-URI of the source image to load and crop.
- `ImageLogoLink` — URL for a secondary "logo" image; stored on the instance (`this.ImageLogoLink`) but not otherwise read/drawn anywhere else in the class (reserved/unused field).
- `nameCanvas` — DOM id of the `<canvas>` element to render into.
- `sizeCanvasWidth` / `sizeCanvasHeight` — display size (CSS px) the canvas should be sized to.
- `is_mobile` — boolean flag enabling mobile touch-offset compensation.

Constructor fields (state):
- `this.imageDataLink`, `this.ImageLogoLink`, `this.nameCanvas` — inputs stored verbatim.
- `this.canvas` — resolved DOM canvas element (set once the image loads).
- `this.groupRect` — `{x, y, w, h}` crop rectangle in canvas pixel coordinates; initialized to a 40px inset from all edges of the canvas.
- `this.currentImage`, `this.logoImage` — unused placeholders (never assigned).
- `this.scaleInfo` — unused scale factor placeholder (`1`).
- `this.ImageLoader`, `this.rectInfo` — unused placeholders (`null`).
- `this.imageData` — an `Image()` used to load `imageDataLink`.
- `this.infoImg` — `{w, h, realW, realH}`: canvas display size and the ratio of natural image size to display size (used to map crop-rect canvas coordinates back to full-resolution source-image coordinates).
- `this.locationClick` — drag-state: `{x, y, selected, clickTop_Left, clickTop_Right, clickBottom_Left, clickBottom_Right}` tracking which crop-handle (if any) is currently being dragged.
- `this.is_mobile`, `this.offsetMobile` — mobile flag and `{left, top}` pixel offset correction applied to touch coordinates.

Methods (all defined as bound arrow-function class fields):
- `initFabricj()` — Loads `this.imageData.src = this.imageDataLink`; on image load, sizes/attaches the target `<canvas>` (by id `nameCanvas`), computes `infoImg.realW/realH` (natural size ÷ display size), stores the canvas element, then calls `setupDataCropRender()` and `initScreenInfo()`. If `is_mobile`, also computes `offsetMobile` based on the `#copy-img-3d-box1` element's width.
- `setupDataCropRender()` — Redraws the base image onto the canvas 2D context, then strokes the current `groupRect` as a red rectangle outline plus four small blue 4×4 squares at its corners (the draggable handle markers).
- `calDistance(w, h)` — Returns squared distance `w*w + h*h` (used for cheap proximity/hit-testing, not a true Euclidean distance).
- `checkLocation(that)` — Clamps `groupRect.w`/`groupRect.h` to a minimum of 5px and to the canvas bounds relative to `groupRect.x`/`groupRect.y`, mutating the object passed in (`that`, always `this`).
- `initScreenInfo()` — Wires up all interaction listeners on `this.canvas`:
  - `selectstart` — prevents default text-selection.
  - `touchstart` — computes touch offset (adjusted by `offsetMobile`), determines (via `calDistance` within a threshold of 128) whether the touch is near one of the four corners, sets the corresponding `locationClick.clickTop_Left/Right/Bottom_Left/Right` flag.
  - `touchmove` — while a corner flag is set, updates `groupRect.x/y/w/h` so the dragged corner follows the touch point (clamped to canvas bounds), calls `checkLocation` then `setupDataCropRender` to redraw.
  - `touchend` / `touchcancel` — clears all corner flags / prevents default.
  - `mousedown` — same corner-proximity detection as `touchstart` but with a threshold of 25 (capture phase).
  - `click` — clears all corner flags (capture phase).
  - `mousemove` — same corner-follow logic as `touchmove`, for mouse dragging (capture phase).
  - `mouseup` — no-op handler (capture phase).
- `exportDataUrl(callBack)` — Creates an off-screen canvas sized to `groupRect.w × groupRect.h`, draws the corresponding sub-region of the *full-resolution* source image (mapping canvas-space `groupRect` back to natural image pixels via `infoImg.realW/realH`), and invokes `callBack(dataUrl)` with the result of `canvas1.toDataURL('image/png', 5.0)` (the quality argument is meaningless for PNG but passed anyway).

---

## src/utils/ImageCuttingLaser.js

**Purpose.** `ImageCuttingLaser` is the core algorithm behind laser-cutting/laser-engraving outline generation. Given the RGBA pixel data of a rendered design (a canvas `ImageData.data` buffer) it builds a binary opaque/transparent mask, grows or shrinks (offsets) that mask's boundary by a configurable margin, traces the resulting boundary into one or more closed polygon outlines, removes redundant/nested outlines, smooths and (optionally) snaps the outline onto externally supplied reference points, and finally emits ready-to-use SVG `<polygon>` strings plus raw point arrays describing the laser cut path(s). It is consumed directly by `src/components/laser-sticker/index.jsx` (and referenced from `src/middleware/draw-tool.js`, `src/containers/Options`, `src/containers/Header`, `src/containers/HeaderMobile`) to compute the vector cut-line around a design so a laser-cutter/sticker product can be produced with an accurate outline and margin around the printed artwork. It internally uses `TraceSkeleton` (`./TraceSkeleton.js`) to compute a topological skeleton when insetting (shrinking) a shape, so that concave/thin regions erode without self-intersecting.

Internally the algorithm represents the working image as a padded 2D grid (`arrayImage`, 5px border on every side so neighbor lookups never go out of bounds) using a small set of sentinel pixel values with informal meaning: `0` = background/outside, `255` = solid/foreground, `100` = "gray" margin/offset ring, `140`/`150` = skeleton point markers (150 = branch/keep, 140 = ordinary skeleton pixel), and various intermediate values (145+) used as thresholds during iterative erosion.

A comment at the end of the file documents the canonical minimal usage pattern:
```js
ImageCuttingLaser data = new ImageCuttingLaser(image.width, image.height);
data.initImageCheck(image.data);
data.addGrayImageArea(14);
data.getVectorFromImage();
data.removeVectorInner();
```

### Exports (default export: class `ImageCuttingLaser`)

Constructor: `new ImageCuttingLaser(width, height)`
- `width`, `height` — nominal pixel dimensions of the source image.
- `this.widthNew = width + 10`, `this.heightNew = height + 10` — padded working grid size (5px border added on each side).
- `this.arrayImage` — array of `Uint8Array` columns (`widthNew` columns × `heightNew` rows) — the main working "image"/mask buffer described above.
- `this.data_point` — array of extracted contour objects, each `{xy: [{x, y}, ...], infoFiuge: number}`; `infoFiuge === -1` means "kept as a top-level/outer outline", any other value records which other contour it was merged/nested into.
- `this.limitInfo` — bounding-box holder; initialized to `[]` and never actually populated by any method (see `getSizeData`) — effectively a vestigial/unused field.

Methods (all bound arrow-function class fields unless noted):

- `initImageVectorPoint(vector)` — Rasterizes a closed polygon given as a flat `[x0,y0,x1,y1,...]` array directly into `arrayImage` by drawing straight connecting segments (including the closing segment from the last point back to the first) between consecutive points using a DDA-style pixel walk, marking each traversed pixel `255`. Coordinates are offset by `+5` for the padding border.
- `initImageCheck(bitMapData, default_compaire=10)` — Clears `arrayImage` to all `0`, then converts a flat RGBA buffer (`bitMapData`, e.g. `ImageData.data`) into a binary mask: for each source pixel, if its alpha channel is below `default_compaire` it's marked `0` (transparent/background), otherwise `255` (opaque/foreground). This is the primary way real image data seeds the algorithm.
- `checkAroundArea(X, Y, value)` — Returns `true` if `arrayImage[X][Y] === 0` and any of its 8 neighbors is `> value` (edge-of-region test used by growth operations).
- `checkEqualArea(X, Y, value)` — Same neighbor test as above but requires the center pixel to equal `value` exactly rather than `0`.
- `checkWhiteArea(X, Y, value)` — Returns `true` if the pixel is `255` and any neighbor is `< value` (used to find the shrinking inward boundary of the white/foreground region during erosion).
- `checkAroundBlachWhite(X, Y)` *(sic — "Black/White")* — Returns `true` if the pixel is `255` and any neighbor isn't `255` (detects the outer boundary pixels of the solid mask).
- `fillimageXY(p1, p2)` — Rasterizes a straight connector line between two points, writing `150` (if current value `>145`) or `140` otherwise along the path — used to fill in gaps between skeleton sample points so the skeleton forms a continuous line.
- `addGrayImageArea(numberPixel)` — Dilates (outsets) the mask boundary outward by `numberPixel` pixels: builds a filled circular stamp of that radius (sampled every 2° around a circle), then for every current boundary pixel (found via `checkAroundBlachWhite`) stamps the circle into `arrayImage`, marking any non-`255` pixel it covers as `100`. This produces the outward cutting margin/bleed.
- `addGrayImageAreaZero()` — One-pixel dilation pass that also marks any pixel adjacent to a `100` pixel as `100`, then zeroes out a 3×3 block around every `100` pixel found — used to erase small/noise margin fragments.
- `addGrayImageAreaNegative1(numberPixel)` — A near-duplicate of `addGrayImageArea` using a slightly different stamp sizing (`numberPixel*2+1`); an alternate/secondary outward-dilation routine using the same circular-stamp technique.
- `addGrayImageAreaNegative(numberPixel)` — Insets (shrinks) the mask inward by `numberPixel` pixels. It iteratively erodes the white region by repeatedly marking `checkWhiteArea` boundary pixels at increasing thresholds, converts the eroded mask to a flat binary array, and calls `TraceSkeleton.trace(arrayImageTraceSkeleton, widthNew, heightNew, 1)` to compute the medial-axis skeleton of the eroded region. It rasterizes the returned skeleton polylines back into `arrayImage` (via `fillimageXY`), then clusters the resulting skeleton pixels into proximity-based groups — "father" groups (junction-like `150` points) and "child" groups (`140` points) — using an iterative nearest-group clustering pass keyed on `numberPixel` distance. Child groups that touch more than one father group (i.e. branch points connecting separate skeleton segments) are kept as part of the new boundary (set to `255`); everything else is zeroed. This produces an eroded outline that avoids self-intersection on concave shapes (unlike naive per-pixel erosion).
- `addGrayImageAreaBorder()` — Simple 1px marker pass: any pixel adjacent to a pixel `>254` (i.e. adjacent to solid `255`) is marked `100`.
- `checkAroundVector(X, Y)` — Returns `true` if the pixel is neither `0` nor `255` (i.e. a "margin"/marked pixel) and has a neighbor equal to `0` — detects the outer edge of the margin ring for contour tracing.
- `checkAroundVectorBorder(X, Y)` — Returns `true` if the pixel is `> 0` and has a neighbor equal to `0` — boundary test used against the post-`addGrayImageArea` mask.
- `checkVectorInnerArrayVector(item, checkIn)` — Given two contour objects (each `{xy:[{x,y},...]}`), returns `true` if any point of `item` is within squared-distance `< 3` of any point of `checkIn` (near-coincidence test used to merge touching/duplicate contours).
- `getVectorFromImage()` — Scans the entire working grid; for every pixel satisfying `checkAroundVectorBorder`, either starts a new contour bucket in `this.data_point` or appends the point to an existing bucket if it's within squared-distance `< 3` of one of that bucket's points (proximity-based contour clustering). Afterward, repeatedly merges any contour whose points are near ("inner" to) another contour's points (via `checkVectorInnerArrayVector`) until no more merges occur. Populates `this.data_point`.
- `getVectorFromImageBorder()` — Same clustering/merge algorithm as `getVectorFromImage`, but using `checkAroundVector` as the per-pixel predicate instead — the parallel implementation used for the "outer border margin" (dilate) pass rather than the inset pass.
- `removeVectorInner()` — Determines which contours in `this.data_point` are nested inside another and discards them. For each candidate root contour it strokes the contour as a path on a scratch `<canvas>` and, using `ctx.isPointInPath(x, y, "nonzero")`, tests what fraction of every *other* contour's points fall inside that stroked path; if fewer than 50% of a contour's points are actually inside, that contour is kept as an independent outline (`infoFiuge` reset to `-1`) rather than discarded as a nested duplicate. Leaves `this.data_point` filtered to the meaningful set of outlines.
- `getpathData()` — For each top-level contour (`infoFiuge === -1`): un-pads coordinates (subtract the 5px border, clamp into `[0, width]`/`[0, height]`), computes a bounding box, then greedily links the contour's unordered points into an ordered path via repeated nearest-neighbor selection (Manhattan distance). When the next-nearest point is farther than the current chain's density allows, the walk starts a fresh sub-path on the remaining points (handles multiple loops/holes found within one blob). Each resulting path is smoothed with a 3-point weighted moving average (weights derived from inverse-squared distance to neighboring points) to produce `pathChange` (path coordinates relative to the bounding box origin, floored to integers). Returns `dataInfo`: an array of `{path: [flat x,y,...], size: {startX, startY, endX, endY}, pathChange: [flat x,y,...]}`.
- `getSizeData()` — Returns `this.limitInfo`. Since `limitInfo` is never populated elsewhere, this effectively always returns `[]` — a vestigial accessor.
- `checkNearPoint(x, y, extend, distance=5)` — Finds the index in `extend` (array of `{x,y}`) with the smallest Manhattan distance to `(x, y)` that is also `< distance`; returns that index, or `-1` if none qualifies.
- `checkNearPointDistance(x, y, extend, offsetDistance)` — Same nearest-point search as `checkNearPoint` but returns `{location, distance}` (both the matched index and its actual distance).
- `lowPathFitter(pointLaser, extend, step=1)` — Smooths a flat path array using a moving-average curve relaxation, run `step` times; treats the path as a closed loop by padding 10 points from each end onto the opposite end before smoothing so the seam blends cleanly. After smoothing, re-snaps every point onto the nearest `extend` reference point if one is close (`checkNearPoint`), strips the padding, and returns the cleaned flat array.
- `getPointLaserPedestal(points)` — Given an ordered array of `{x,y}` points, inserts integer-stepped intermediate points along each straight segment between consecutive points (a Bresenham-like densification), returning a densely sampled point array.
- `getPointLaserPedestal1(points)` — Same densification as `getPointLaserPedestal`, but rounds every output coordinate to 5 decimal places.
- `getPointLaserPedestalNear(points)` — Used for "pedestal" (stand/base) style laser items: computes the bounding box of `points`, then for the first and last points (each expected to carry a companion `x_near`/`y_near` coordinate) densifies the 2-point segment to its near-companion via `getPointLaserPedestal1` and assigns each interpolated point a `distance` weight (`sqrt` of its offset from the segment midpoint, floored at 1.5/3). Returns the concatenation of the left-side and right-side densified segments.
- `memovePointLaserPedestalNear(points, distance)` *(sic)* — Computes the y-bounding box of `points`; if the vertical span exceeds `distance`, filters out all points within `distance` of the bottom edge; otherwise returns `points` unchanged.
- `fillterLocationExtendPointDistance(svgInfo, pointerLaser, offsetDistance=5)` *(sic — "filter…")* — For each point in a flat `svgInfo` path, finds the nearest point in `pointerLaser` within `offsetDistance` (via `checkNearPointDistance`) and, if found, snaps the `svgInfo` coordinate onto it. Rounds all coordinates to 4 decimals. Returns the modified `svgInfo`.
- `fillterLocationExtendPoint(svgInfo, pointerLaser, offsetDistance=5)` *(sic)* — More elaborate reference-snapping pass: marks each `pointerLaser` point with bookkeeping fields (`pick`, `loc`), snaps nearby `svgInfo` points onto them (consuming the reference point), then re-inserts any *unconsumed* `pointerLaser` reference points back into `svgInfo` at their correct sequential position (inferred from surrounding index ordering) so the final path is guaranteed to pass through every reference point, not just the ones a traced point happened to land near. Removes trailing duplicate points and rounds to 4 decimals. Returns the augmented `svgInfo`.
- `removeDuplicatePoint(pointerLaser, extend=[])` — Cleans a flat path of redundant/collinear points: rounds coordinates to 3 decimals, then classifies interior points (using several geometric pattern checks against immediate neighbors — axis-aligned runs, diagonal midpoints, etc., tagged with sentinel codes `-1`..`-4`) as redundant unless they're near an `extend` reference point. Runs the surviving points through `lowPathFitter`, then straight-line-interpolates across the spans of removed/redundant points so the path stays geometrically continuous. Returns the cleaned array (same length; redundant points are repositioned via interpolation rather than physically deleted).
- `checkLaserInval(pointerLaser)` *(sic — "…Invalid")* — Returns `true` only if the path array has more than 10 elements and none of them is `NaN`; otherwise `false`. Used as an output validity gate.
- `getInfoDataVectorLaser(data, sizeInfo, extend=[], smooth=false, setupInfo)` — **Primary public entry point.** Builds the binary mask from `data` (RGBA buffer, threshold 50 via `initImageCheck`), then either insets (`sizeInfo < 0`: `addGrayImageAreaNegative(-sizeInfo)` + `addGrayImageArea(1)`) or dilates (`sizeInfo > 0`: `addGrayImageArea(sizeInfo)`) the boundary, extracts contours (`getVectorFromImage`), removes nested duplicates (`removeVectorInner`), and converts them to ordered paths (`getpathData`). Scales every path from raw pixel space into physical output units using `setupInfo.border.width / setupInfo.image.width`. If `smooth` is `true`, further cleans each path with `removeDuplicatePoint` → `lowPathFitter` → `fillterLocationExtendPoint` (snapping onto `extend`, with `offsetDistance` widened when `setupInfo.scaleOfCache` is present) and recomputes each path's bounding box. Builds an inline `<svg>` string containing a `<polygon>` (with a boilerplate linear-gradient fill style) for each surviving path. Returns `svgInfo`: an array of `{size: {startX, startY, endX, endY, leftW, topH, left, top}, dataSvg: "<svg>...</svg>", pointerLaser: [flat x,y,...]}`, filtered through `checkLaserInval`. Resets `arrayImage`, `data_point`, `limitInfo` to empty before returning. This is the method actually invoked by `src/components/laser-sticker/index.jsx` (`imageData.getInfoDataVectorLaser(map.data, Math.floor(mmLaser), extend, true, setupInfo)`) to compute the laser cut-line for a design.
- `getInfoDataVectorLaserPedestal(data, sizeInfo, extend=[], smooth=false, setupInfo, extend1=[])` — Variant of `getInfoDataVectorLaser` for pedestal/base-style laser items. Reads an optional alpha threshold from `setupInfo.default_compaire` (default 10). Runs the same inset/dilate → trace → declutter pipeline, but when `smooth` is true it computes `offsetDistance` from the vertical span of `extend`, and runs a more elaborate multi-pass sequence of `removeDuplicatePoint` / `lowPathFitter` / `fillterLocationExtendPoint` / `fillterLocationExtendPointDistance` against *two* reference outlines (`extend` and `extend1`, both optionally pre-scaled by `setupInfo.scaleOfCache`) to snap the path onto both the main silhouette and the pedestal seam. Filters results by `pathChange.length > 30` (rather than `checkLaserInval`). Returns the same `svgInfo` shape as `getInfoDataVectorLaser`.
- `getpathDataBorder()` — Simplified sibling of `getpathData`: orders each top-level contour's points via the same nearest-neighbor walk (without the sub-path-splitting branch) and applies the same weighted-smoothing pass to produce `pathChange`. Used by the "border" pipeline.
- `getInfoDataVectorLaserBorder(sizeInfo)` — Simplified alternate entry point: dilates the *already-populated* `arrayImage` by `sizeInfo` (`addGrayImageArea`), extracts and cleans contours (`getVectorFromImage`, `removeVectorInner`), builds path data via `getpathDataBorder`, and returns an array of `{size: {..., left, top, leftW, topH}, pointerLaser: [flat path]}` (no SVG string is built). Unlike `getInfoDataVectorLaser`, it does not call `initImageCheck` itself — it assumes the mask was already seeded by a prior call.
- `checkAroundBlackPoint(X, Y)` — Helper for `getImageDataInfoToPreview`: returns `false` if the pixel is `>99`; for a `0`-valued pixel, returns `false` immediately if any neighbor is `>155` (would create a visible artifact), and returns `true` if at least one neighbor is exactly `100` (adjacent to a margin pixel).
- `getImageDataInfoToPreview(data)` — Builds a cleaned/smoothed binary preview mask from raw RGBA `data`: seeds the mask (`initImageCheck`), stamps a 9×9 `100`-valued block around every boundary pixel (`checkAroundBlachWhite`), reclassifies pixels neighboring a `100` pixel as `155` (`checkAroundBlackPoint`), then collapses `155 → 255` and everything else `< 155 → 0`. Returns a flat row-major array (cropped back to the original `width`×`height`, excluding the 5px padding) of `0`/`255` values — used to show the user a preview silhouette without necessarily running the full vector-tracing pipeline.

---

## src/utils/alert-screen.js

**Purpose.** Provides a single helper that shows a full-image "2D preview" modal (using the SweetAlert2 library, `sweetalert2`) for a rendered design, auto-scaling the popup width to fit the current viewport and device type (mobile vs. desktop/tablet).

### Module-level constants
- `DEFAULT_SCALE_2D_PC = 0.86` — width scale factor applied on desktop.
- `DEFAULT_SCALE_2D_MOBLIE = 0.6` *(sic)* — screen aspect-ratio threshold used to decide whether a mobile device should be treated as tablet-sized.
- `DEFAULT_SCALE_2D_TABLET = 0.86` — width scale factor applied when a mobile device's screen is "tablet-like" (aspect ratio above the mobile threshold).

### Exports
- `show2DPreviewDetail(imageData, is_mobile)` — Computes a popup width (`text_size_image`) from the smaller of `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight`. If `imageData.data` is non-null: computes `scale_size_screen` (min dimension ÷ max dimension of the viewport) to decide, for mobile devices, whether to apply the tablet scale factor; for non-mobile it always applies the PC scale factor. It then recomputes `scale_size_screen` as `min(viewportWidth/imageData.width, viewportHeight/imageData.height)`, multiplies by `imageData.width` and the chosen scale factor to get the final popup pixel width. Builds an `<img class='img-2d-responsive' src='{imageData.data}' onerror='alert(1)'>` tag (or a "not available" message if `imageData.data` is null) and calls `sww.fire({...})` (SweetAlert2) to display it in a modal with a close button, a cancel button labeled `閉じる` (Japanese for "Close"), and CSS classes `sww-contai-product-detail-close-button` / `sww-content-product-detail preview-2d-content` / `preview-2d-container`. No return value.

---

## src/utils/convertFullsizeToHaftsize.js

**Purpose.** Single-purpose text-normalization helper: converts full-width (Zenkaku) Latin characters/punctuation (used in Japanese input, code points `！`-`～`, i.e. U+FF01–U+FF5E) into their standard half-width (Hankaku) ASCII equivalents, by subtracting the fixed full-width/half-width Unicode offset `0xFEE0` from each matching character's code point.

### Exports
- `convertFullsizeToHaftsize(value)` *(default export; sic — "Halfsize")* — Takes a string `value`, replaces every full-width character in the `！`-`～` range with its corresponding ASCII character via `String.fromCharCode(charCode - 0xFEE0)`, and returns the converted string. Pure function, no side effects.

---

## src/utils/cookieStorage.js

**Purpose.** Minimal cookie read/write helpers used for persisting small values (e.g. session tokens) in `document.cookie`.

### Exports
- `setCookie(name, value)` — Writes a cookie named `name` with URI-encoded `value`, an expiry 10 years in the future, and `path=/`. No return value.
- `getCookie(name)` — URI-decodes `document.cookie`, splits it on `;`, and returns the value portion of the first entry whose trimmed name matches `name + "="`. Returns `null` if not found.

---

## src/utils/csg-worker.js

**Purpose.** Sets up an on-demand Web Worker for running CSG (Constructive Solid Geometry) boolean operations (union/subtract/intersect, from `./csg-lib.js`) off the main thread, and monkey-patches a `CSG.doAsync` method onto the imported `CSG` object so the rest of the app can request an async CSG op without knowing about the worker plumbing. The worker's script is built at runtime by fetching the *source text* of `../csg-lib.js`, stripping its trailing `export` statement, and appending a small `onmessage` handler, then loading that as a `Blob` URL — so the worker runs the same CSG implementation as the main thread, self-contained.

### Module-level state
- `gWorkersStarted` — boolean flag, `true` once the worker has begun initializing.
- `gWorker` — `{worker: Worker, busy: boolean}` holding the single spawned worker instance.
- `gWorkerUrl` — the `Blob` object URL the worker was constructed from.
- `taskId` — monotonically incrementing id used to correlate requests/responses.
- `tasks` — map of `taskId → task` objects (`{a, op, b, taskId, resolve, result}`) awaiting a response from the worker.

### Internal helpers (not exported directly, but drive the side effect below)
- `spawnWorker()` — Creates a `new Worker(gWorkerUrl)`, wires its `onmessage` to look up the pending task by `taskId`, resolve it with `CSG.fromJSON(result)`, mark the worker free, and returns the `{worker, busy}` record (stored in `gWorker`).
- `getWorker()` — Lazily fetches `../csg-lib.js`'s source text (once, guarded by `gWorkersStarted`), builds and registers the Blob worker script, and spawns the worker; on subsequent calls, if the existing worker is idle it's claimed (marked busy) and returned via a thenable wrapper, otherwise a no-op thenable is returned (meaning: if the single worker is already busy, the request is effectively dropped/ignored — there is no queueing).

### Exports
- Default export: `{}` (an empty object — the module is imported purely for its side effect of patching `CSG.doAsync`).
- Side effect: `CSG.doAsync = (a, op, b) => Promise` — queues (or drops, if the sole worker is busy) a task `{a, op, b, taskId}`, posts it to the worker as JSON, and returns a `Promise` that resolves with `CSG.fromJSON(result)` once the worker responds with `CSG.fromJSON(a)[op](CSG.fromJSON(b))`. `op` is expected to be a `CSG` instance method name (e.g. `"union"`, `"subtract"`, `"intersect"`).

---

## src/utils/escapeJSON.js

**Purpose.** Trivial wrapper around `JSON.stringify` (a commented-out block shows an earlier, abandoned manual-escaping implementation using `\n`/`\r`/`\t` replacement).

### Exports
- `escapeJSON(string)` (default export) — Returns `JSON.stringify(string)`.

---

## src/utils/fontdetect.js

**Purpose.** A classic canvas/DOM-free web-font-loading detector (singleton IIFE-built object `FontDetect`). It measures the rendered width of a sample string set in a target font (falling back to generic families) and compares it against the width when only the fallback families are used — if the widths ever diverge across all fallback families, the target font is considered loaded. Used to detect when a custom/Google font has finished downloading before using it on the canvas (e.g. before rendering text in the design editor).

### Module-level state (closure variables)
- `_isInitialized` — whether the hidden measurement DOM (`#fontdetectHelper` div/span) has been created.
- `_aFallbackFonts` — `['serif', 'sans-serif', 'monospace', 'cursive', 'fantasy']`, the generic families tested against.
- `span` — the hidden `<span>` element used for width measurement.
- `eLang` — current sample-text language key (default `'en'`).
- `aChars` — map of language → sample character string; only `en` (`'abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyzABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ'`) is defined.

### Exports (default export: the `FontDetect` singleton object)
- `onFontLoaded(p_cssFontName, p_onLoad, p_onFail, p_options)` — Polls (via `setInterval`, default every `p_options.msInterval || 100` ms) whether `p_cssFontName` is loaded using `isFontLoaded`. Calls `p_onLoad(p_cssFontName)` as soon as it's detected loaded (or immediately, synchronously, if already loaded); calls `p_onFail(p_cssFontName)` if `p_options.msTimeout || 2000` ms elapse without detection. `p_options.eLang` selects the sample-character set (falls back to `'en'` if not a recognized key). No-op if `p_cssFontName` is falsy or neither callback is supplied. Lazily calls internal `_init()` on first use to build the hidden measurement DOM.
- `isFontLoaded(p_cssFontName)` — For each fallback family, sets `span.style.fontFamily = '"<font>",<fallback>'` and records `span.offsetWidth`; if the width differs between fallback families, the target font must be rendering distinctly (i.e. it's loaded), returning `true`; if all fallback widths are identical, returns `false` (font not yet available / not distinguishable from fallbacks).
- `whichFont(p_element)` — Reads the computed `font-family` stack of `p_element` (via `getComputedStyle`, or jQuery `.css()` if `p_element` isn't a DOM `Element` and `window.jQuery` exists), and walks the comma-separated family list, returning the first entry that either is itself a generic fallback name or that `isFontLoaded` confirms is actually loaded. Returns `null` if none match.

---

## src/utils/onbeforeunload.js

**Purpose.** Installs a `beforeunload` confirmation prompt ("Do you want to reload this site?") on module load, and exports a function to remove that listener again (e.g. once a design has been saved and the warning is no longer needed).

### Exports
- `disableOnBeforeUnload()` (default export) — Removes the `onBeforeUnload` listener that was attached to `window` when the module was first imported, via `window.removeEventListener('beforeunload', onBeforeUnload)`.

### Side effect on import
- Immediately calls `window.addEventListener('beforeunload', onBeforeUnload)`, where `onBeforeUnload(event)` sets `event.returnValue` to a confirmation string and returns it (standard browser "leave site?" prompt pattern; most modern browsers show a generic built-in message rather than this custom text).

---

## src/utils/queryloader.js

**Purpose.** Resolves the initial "draw parameters" query string used to configure the editor on load, preferring the current URL's query string and falling back to a previously stored copy in `sessionStorage`/cookies (so a reload or return visit can restore the same session/design parameters even after the URL's query string has been stripped, e.g. by `sessionHelper.persistSession`).

### Exports
- `loadQueryString(value=null)` (default export) — Ignores the `value` parameter's initial content (it's immediately overwritten) and instead reads `window.location.search.substring(1)`. If that's `null`/`undefined`/shorter than 4 characters, it instead calls the internal `getLocalData("DRAWPARAM")` and, if found, un-escapes `%26`→`&` and `%3D`→`=` in the stored string. Returns the resolved query string (or `null`/`undefined` if nothing is available).

### Internal (non-exported) helpers
- `getLocalData(name)` — Reads `sessionStorage.getItem(name)`; if present and longer than 4 characters, returns it. Otherwise falls back to parsing `document.cookie` for a `name=` entry and returns its value, or `null` if not found in either place.
- `setLocalData(name, value)` — Writes to `sessionStorage.setItem(name, value)` (a commented-out block shows an earlier cookie-based implementation that was replaced).

---

## src/utils/renderCanvas.js

**Purpose.** A constructor-style helper (invoked with `.call`/`new` against some outer context, given its use of `this.canvas`/`this.FabricCanvas`/`this.createCanvas`) that renders a Fabric.js design (loaded from `DrawTool.sides._collection[index]`, i.e. the vendored `drawtool.js` 2D editor's per-side design state) onto an off-screen `<canvas>` and assigns that canvas as the texture map of a Three.js mesh material — used to project the 2D design canvas onto the 3D product preview mesh. Imports `DrawTool` from `../../draw-tool/drawtool` and `fabric` from the `fabric` package. Note: it references `THREE.MeshPhongMaterial`/`THREE.DoubleSide` without importing `THREE` in this file, so it depends on `THREE` being available as a global in whatever scope this function is invoked from.

### Exports
- `renderCanvas(mesh, index)` (default export, a `function` intended to be called with a bound `this`) — Creates `this.canvas = document.createElement('canvas')` and defines two members on `this`:
  - `initFabric()` — Creates `this.FabricCanvas = new fabric.Canvas(this.canvas, {selection:false, enableRetinaScaling:true, preserveObjectStacking:true})`. (Defined but not automatically invoked within `renderCanvas` itself.)
  - `createCanvas(mesh, index)` — Sets global Fabric object defaults (`transparentCorners:false`, custom corner styling), creates a **new** `fabric.Canvas('c')` (hard-coded DOM id `'c'`, independent of `this.canvas`), loads the JSON design of `DrawTool.sides._collection[index]` (via its `toJSON()`) into that canvas, and — once loaded — assigns `mesh.material = new THREE.MeshPhongMaterial({map: canvas, specular: 0x111100})` with `alphaTest = 0.5` and `side = THREE.DoubleSide`, i.e. it uses the *Fabric canvas element itself* as the Three.js texture map. Contains a large commented-out block showing an earlier approach that manually copied the design into a separate mapping canvas and rotated it before building a `THREE.Texture`.

---

## src/utils/sessionHelper.js

**Purpose.** Persists the current session identifier across the app: updates the shared `query` object (`src/constants/query`), rewrites the `DRAWPARAM` string cached in `sessionStorage` so a page reload keeps the same session, optionally pings a companion "web" host to sync the session server-side (via a 1×1 image request, avoiding CORS/navigation), and scrubs the `session` parameter out of the visible URL (via `history.replaceState`) so it isn't left exposed in the address bar while still being retained in storage.

### Exports
- `persistSession(session)` — No-ops if `session` is falsy. Sets `query.session = session` (mutates the shared `query` singleton from `../constants/query`). Reads the existing `DRAWPARAM` value from `sessionStorage`; if it already contains a `session=`/`session%3D` segment (matched via regex `/(session(?:=|%3D))([^&%]*)/`), replaces that segment's value in place, otherwise appends a new `session=<value>` (or `session%3D<value>`) segment, choosing `&` vs `%26` as separator based on what the existing string already uses. Writes the updated value back to `sessionStorage.setItem("DRAWPARAM", drawParam)`. If the `WEBHOST` constant (from `../constants`) is set, creates a throw-away `Image()` whose `src` is `${WEBHOST}/login.php?user_session=${session}` purely to fire the request as a side effect (image response is never used). Finally, tries to strip the `session` query parameter from `window.location` via the `URL` API and `history.replaceState`, swallowing/logging any error. No return value.

---

## src/utils/csg-lib.js

**Purpose.** Implements Constructive Solid Geometry (CSG) boolean operations (union, subtract, intersect) on arbitrary 3D solids using a BSP-tree (binary space partition) algorithm. It defines an engine-agnostic solid-geometry core: `CSG` (a bag of `Polygon`s with boolean-op methods), `Vector` (minimal 3D vector math), `Vertex` (position + normal + optional uv/color, with `flip()`/`interpolate()` for polygon splitting), `Plane` (splits polygons against a plane), and `Node` (a BSP-tree node used internally to implement union/subtract/intersect). This file has no dependency on Three.js — it operates purely on its own plain-object vector/vertex/polygon types, which is what lets it also run unmodified inside a Web Worker.

**Vendored: yes** — header states "Copyright (c) 2011 Evan Wallace (http://madebyevan.com/), under the MIT license. THREE.js rework by thrax." This is the classic `csg.js` BSP-CSG library by Evan Wallace, adapted/reworked for Three.js by a contributor "thrax" (a known community fork of csg.js).

### Exports

`export {CSG, Vertex, Vector, Polygon, Plane}`

- `class CSG` — constructor takes no args (`this.polygons = []`).
  - `union(csg)`, `subtract(csg)`, `intersect(csg)` — combine `this` with another `CSG` instance via BSP-tree clipping; each returns a **new** `CSG` (inputs are cloned internally, not mutated).
  - `clone()`, `toPolygons()`, `inverse()`.
  - Static `CSG.fromPolygons(polygons)` — builds a `CSG` from an array of `Polygon`.
  - Static `CSG.fromJSON(json)` — deserializes a plain `{polygons:[...]}` struct (as produced by `JSON.stringify`) back into a real `CSG`/`Polygon`/`Vertex` object graph; used to pass CSG data to/from a Web Worker.
- `class Vertex` — `constructor(pos, normal, uv, color)`; wraps position/normal (and optional uv/color) as `Vector`s; used by `three-csg.js` when building polygons from Three.js geometry.
- `class Polygon` — `constructor(vertices, shared)`; `shared` is an arbitrary tag (e.g. material/group index) carried through clipping/splitting operations; used by `three-csg.js` (`new Polygon(vertices, objectIndex)`).
- `class Vector`, `class Plane`, `class Node` — internal supporting math/BSP types; not referenced directly by any app code.

**Relationship to three-csg.js / csg-worker.js (same directory):**
- `three-csg.js` does `import {CSG, Vertex, Polygon} from "./csg-lib.js"` and then monkey-patches additional static methods (`fromGeometry`, `fromMesh`, `toGeometry`, `toMesh`) directly onto csg-lib's `CSG` class, then re-exports that same (now Three.js-aware) `CSG` as its own default export.
- `three-csg.js` also does `import "./csg-worker.js"` purely for its side effect. `csg-worker.js` itself does `import {CSG} from "./csg-lib.js"` and adds a further static method `CSG.doAsync(a, op, b)`, which spins up a Web Worker (by `fetch`-ing and re-executing the raw source text of `csg-lib.js` inside a Blob URL) to run `CSG.fromJSON(a)[op](CSG.fromJSON(b))` off the main thread and resolve a Promise with the result.
- **In practice, `CSG.doAsync` is never called anywhere in the app** (grepped across all of `src`) — the app performs boolean ops synchronously on the main thread via the plain `.subtract()` instance method. `csg-worker.js`'s worker-offload machinery is present but currently a dead/unused code path.
- Net effect: `csg-lib.js` is never imported directly by application components — it is only reached indirectly through `three-csg.js`.

---

## src/utils/three-csg.js

**Purpose.** A thin Three.js integration layer over `csg-lib.js`'s pure BSP-CSG engine. It adds the glue needed to go from Three.js `Mesh`/`Geometry`/`BufferGeometry` objects into the engine's `CSG` representation and back: `CSG.fromGeometry`/`CSG.fromMesh` convert a Three.js mesh (its world transform applied) into a `CSG` solid, and `CSG.toGeometry`/`CSG.toMesh` convert a `CSG` solid's polygon soup back into a `THREE.BufferGeometry`/`THREE.Mesh` (with computed bounding volumes and shadow flags). This is the module the app actually imports to perform mesh boolean operations (e.g. cutting a hole for laser-engraving previews).

**Vendored: yes** — no explicit version banner, but the header comment (`//THREE.js rework by thrax`, matching the neighboring `csg-lib.js`) and the `import {CSG, Vertex, Polygon} from "./csg-lib.js"` pattern identify it as the Three.js mesh-conversion companion to the same community `csg.js` (Evan Wallace) rework. It imports Three.js via the `three` npm package (`import * as THREE from 'three'`).

### Exports

`export default CSG` — the same `CSG` class from `csg-lib.js`, augmented with the following static methods (module has the side effect of mutating/extending the imported class):

- `CSG.fromMesh(mesh, objectIndex)` — converts a `THREE.Mesh` (Geometry or BufferGeometry) into a `CSG` solid, applying the mesh's local `matrix` to bake in position/rotation/scale. `objectIndex` (optional) is stamped onto each resulting `Polygon.shared`. **This is the method the app calls** — e.g. `bspA = CSG.fromMesh(mesh)`, `bspB = CSG.fromMesh(mesh_hole)` in `src/components/3d-laser-preview/index.jsx`, called with only the mesh (no `objectIndex`).
- `CSG.toMesh(csg, toMatrix = new THREE.Matrix4(), toMaterial)` — converts a `CSG` solid back into a `THREE.Mesh`, building a `BufferGeometry` (default buffered path), applying the inverse of `toMatrix` to the geometry then setting it back as the mesh's transform, recomputing bounding sphere/box, and enabling `castShadow`/`receiveShadow`. **Called by the app as** `meshResult = CSG.toMesh(bspResult, mesh.matrix, mesh.material)` to rebuild a mesh after a boolean subtraction, reusing the original mesh's matrix and material.
- `CSG.fromGeometry(geom, objectIndex)` / `CSG.toGeometry(csg, buffered=true)` — lower-level geometry-only variants used internally by `fromMesh`/`toMesh`; not called directly by app code. (Note: the `buffered=false` legacy path references a `Geometry` class that isn't imported in this file — that branch would throw if ever invoked; only the default buffered/BufferGeometry path is exercised by any current caller.)

**Actual app usage** (confirmed via `src/components/3d-laser-preview/index.jsx`, `import CSG from '../../utils/three-csg.js'`): the standard pattern is

```js
bspA = CSG.fromMesh(mesh);
bspB = CSG.fromMesh(mesh_hole);
bspResult = bspA.subtract(bspB);          // instance method inherited from csg-lib.js's CSG
meshResult = CSG.toMesh(bspResult, mesh.matrix, mesh.material);
```

used to cut engraving/hole geometry out of a base mesh for the 3D laser preview. `union`/`intersect` (also available on `CSG` instances) are not used anywhere in the app — only `subtract`.

---

## src/utils/DRACOLoader.js

**Purpose.** A Three.js `Loader` subclass that decodes Draco-compressed geometry buffers (positions/normals/colors/UVs/indices) into a `THREE.BufferGeometry`, offloading the actual decode work to a pool of Web Workers running the Google Draco WASM/JS decoder (fetched at `decoderPath`). It manages worker lifecycle (spawn up to `workerLimit` workers, load-balance tasks across them, cache in-flight decode tasks per input buffer via a `WeakMap`, and release/terminate workers on `dispose()`).

**Vendored: yes** — this is Three.js's official `examples/jsm/loaders/DRACOLoader.js`, imported here as `import { BufferAttribute, BufferGeometry, FileLoader, Loader } from 'three'`. The file's API shape (instance methods `setDecoderPath`/`setDecoderConfig`/`setWorkerLimit`, deprecated static shims like `DRACOLoader.setDecoderPath` that just `console.warn`, and use of `ColorManagement`-era three.js internals) indicates a notably newer three.js revision than the project's pinned npm dependency (`"three": "^0.124.0"` in `package.json`) — i.e. this loader was vendored from a later release of three.js and dropped in alongside the older installed package.

### Exports

`export { DRACOLoader };`

- `DRACOLoader(manager)` — constructor; `manager` is an optional `THREE.LoadingManager` (passed through to `Loader`). Sets default `decoderPath = ''`, `workerLimit = 4`, and default Draco attribute-ID/type maps.
- Public instance methods actually exercised by this codebase (via `src/utils/GLTFLoader.js`, which is the real internal consumer):
  - `preload()` — pre-fetches/instantiates the decoder and pre-warms the worker pool; called once by `GLTFLoader` right after a `DRACOLoader` is registered (`this.dracoLoader.preload()`).
  - `decodeDracoFile(buffer, callback, attributeIDs, attributeTypes)` *(deprecated but still the one actually called)* — decodes an `ArrayBuffer` of Draco-compressed data and invokes `callback(bufferGeometry)` when done; called by `GLTFLoader`'s Draco mesh-compression extension per primitive (`dracoLoader.decodeDracoFile(bufferView, function(geometry){...})`).
  - Also present but not observed to be called by app/GLTFLoader code in this repo: `load(url, onLoad, onProgress, onError)`, `setDecoderPath(path)`, `setDecoderConfig(config)`, `setWorkerLimit(n)`, `decodeGeometry(buffer, taskConfig)`, `dispose()`.

**Actual app usage:** `src/components/3d-triple/index.jsx` does `import { DRACOLoader } from "../../utils/DRACOLoader.js"`, then:

```js
const dracoLoader = new DRACOLoader();
this.loader.setDRACOLoader(dracoLoader);   // this.loader is a GLTFLoader
```

The application itself only constructs a `DRACOLoader` and hands it to `GLTFLoader.setDRACOLoader()`. It never calls `preload()`/`decodeDracoFile()` etc. directly — those are invoked internally by `src/utils/GLTFLoader.js` (also vendored, in the same directory) whenever a loaded `.glb`/`.gltf` model contains Draco-compressed mesh data.

---

## src/utils/OrbitControls.js

**Purpose.** Standard Three.js camera controls providing mouse/touch/keyboard orbit (rotate around a target), dolly/zoom, and pan, while preserving the camera's "up" vector — the same controls used across virtually all three.js example scenes. It attaches DOM event listeners (mouse, wheel, touch, keydown) to a target element and updates the attached camera's position/orientation via spherical coordinates each frame when `update()` is called.

**Vendored: yes** — header authorship comment lists `@author qiao`, `@author mrdoob`, `@author alteredq`, `@author WestLangley`, `@author erich666`, which is the exact author list of Three.js's official `examples/js/controls/OrbitControls.js` / `examples/jsm/controls/OrbitControls.js`. Imports Three.js via `import * as THREE from 'three'`.

### Exports

`export default function OrbitControls(object, domElement)` — a constructor function (not an ES class); `OrbitControls.prototype = Object.create(THREE.EventDispatcher.prototype)`, so instances are event dispatchers but are **not** `Object3D`s (they have no `children` array by default).

- `new OrbitControls(camera, domElement)` — `camera` is the `THREE.Camera` to control; `domElement` is the DOM element to attach input listeners to. Used identically in all three consumers (`src/components/3d-preview/index.jsx`, `src/components/3d-triple/index.jsx`, `src/components/3d-laser-preview/index.jsx`):
  ```js
  this.controls = new OrbitControls(this.camera, this.renderer.domElement);
  this.controls.maxPolarAngle = Math.PI;
  this.controls.minPolarAngle = 0;
  this.controls.enableDamping = true;
  this.controls.enablePan = false;
  this.controls.dampingFactor = 0.1;
  this.controls.rotateSpeed = 0.1;
  this.controls.autoRotate = false;
  this.controls.autoRotateSpeed = 0.01;
  this.controls.update();
  ```
- Public config properties actually set by the app (plain fields, not methods): `maxPolarAngle`, `minPolarAngle`, `enableDamping`, `enablePan`, `dampingFactor`, `rotateSpeed`, `autoRotate`, `autoRotateSpeed`. (Many other config fields exist — `minDistance`/`maxDistance`, `minZoom`/`maxZoom`, `minAzimuthAngle`/`maxAzimuthAngle`, `enableZoom`, `zoomSpeed`, `enableRotate`, `screenSpacePanning`, `panSpeed`, `keyPanSpeed`, `enableKeys`, `keys`, `mouseButtons`, `target` — none of these are touched by the app.)
- `update()` — recomputes camera position from internal spherical coordinates/damping state; must be called every animation frame (and after any manual property change). Called each render-loop tick in all three consuming components.
- `dispose()` — removes all DOM event listeners (`contextmenu`, `mousedown`, `wheel`, `touchstart/end/move`, `mousemove`, `mouseup`, `keydown`). Called on component teardown in `3d-preview/index.jsx` and `3d-laser-preview/index.jsx`.
- Present but not used by any consumer: `getPolarAngle()`, `getAzimuthalAngle()`, `saveState()`, `reset()`, plus several deprecated getter/setter aliases (`noZoom`, `noRotate`, `noPan`, `noKeys`, `staticMoving`, `dynamicDampingFactor`) that just `console.warn` and forward to the modern properties.
- Note: `3d-preview/index.jsx` and `3d-laser-preview/index.jsx` also guard `if (this.controls.children != undefined) { while (this.controls.children.length > 0) {...this.controls.remove(obj)...} }` before calling `dispose()` — but since `OrbitControls` instances never have a `children` property (they inherit from `EventDispatcher`, not `Object3D`), this branch is effectively dead/no-op code in practice.

---

## src/utils/OBJExporter.js

**Purpose.** Serializes a Three.js `Object3D` scene graph (traversing `Mesh`, `Line`/`LineSegments`, and `Points` children) into Wavefront `.obj` text format — emitting `o` (object name), `v` (vertex), `vt` (UV), `vn` (normal), `f` (face), `l` (line), and `p` (point) records, applying each object's `matrixWorld` so exported coordinates are in world space.

**Vendored: yes** — this is Three.js's official `examples/jsm/exporters/OBJExporter.js`. Imports `{ Color, ColorManagement, Matrix3, SRGBColorSpace, Vector2, Vector3 }` from `'three'`; the use of `ColorManagement`/`SRGBColorSpace` (three.js's color-management API, introduced well after the project's pinned `three@^0.124.0`) again indicates this file was vendored from a newer three.js release than the installed npm package, similar to `DRACOLoader.js`.

### Exports

`export { OBJExporter };`

- `class OBJExporter` — no constructor arguments.
  - `parse(object)` — the sole public method. Walks `object.traverse(...)`, converting every descendant with `isMesh`/`isLine`/`isPoints` true into OBJ records, and returns the complete `.obj` file contents as a single string.

**Actual app usage:** `src/components/3d-triple/index.jsx` imports it (`import { OBJExporter } from '../../utils/OBJExporter.js'`) and instantiates it once in the constructor: `this.objExport = new OBJExporter();`. However, based on a full search of the file, **`.parse()` is never called** — `this.objExport` is otherwise unreferenced. The component's actual "export to OBJ" feature (`exportToOBJ(scene)`, used when building the downloadable `model.obj` inside a zip) is a separate, hand-written OBJ serializer implemented directly in `3d-triple/index.jsx`, not a call into this vendored `OBJExporter` class. So this file is currently imported/instantiated but effectively dead code in the app's runtime path.

---

## src/utils/MethodImageProcessing.js

**Purpose.** `MethodImageProcessing` (default export, a stateful class) performs raster/pixel-level analysis and transformation for the 2D `drawtool.js` design canvas, primarily to support laser-cutting and sticker/acrylic-stand workflows. Its core capabilities are: flood-fill based connected-component (blob) detection on transparent-background artwork, generating "bridge" connectors between disconnected ink regions so cut/stencil shapes stay physically joined, computing laser-safe cut/hole border paths (in cm-space) from Fabric.js item geometry (delegating polygon simplification to `ImageCuttingLaser`), and validating that laser-cut holes (e.g. for hanging chains) aren't placed too close to cut lines (showing a SweetAlert2 warning via `Swal` when they are). It works together with `DrawTool` (item/border metadata) and `ImageCuttingLaser` (vector path simplification for laser output) and is consumed by higher-level export/preview code that prepares final designs for cutting, printing, or 3D preview.

Two non-exported module-level helper functions are used throughout the class: `maxArr(arr)` and `minArr(arr)`, which return the maximum/minimum numeric value in an array (simple loop-based reductions, used for bounding-box calculations).

### Exports

Default export: `class MethodImageProcessing`

**Constructor** — `constructor()`
- `this.canvasInfo` — initialized to `null`; later lazily set to an offscreen `<canvas>` element by `initCanvas()` and reused as scratch space by several pixel-processing methods (`checkImageMask`, `checkImageMaskRegion`, `checkHoleNearCutting`).

**Methods**

- `initCanvas()` — creates a detached `<canvas>` element via `document.createElement` and stores it on `this.canvasInfo` for later reuse.
- `addImageProcess(img)` — returns a Promise that resolves to `true` once `img.onload` fires; also sets `img.crossOrigin = 'anonymous'`. Does not attach an error handler, so a failed image load leaves the promise pending indefinitely.
- `getRotatedBBox(region)` — given an array of `{x,y}` points, computes their centroid and the angle of the line from the first to second point, rotates all points to align that angle with the x-axis, then measures the axis-aligned bounding box of the rotated points. Returns `{width, height, angle}` (angle in degrees) — an oriented (minimum-rotation) bounding box approximation used to size connector strokes between blobs.
- `checkImageMask(base64)` *(async)* — loads a base64 image onto `this.canvasInfo`, first calls `privateAreaYellowing` to flag enclosed transparent "holes" in yellow, then flood-fills all alpha>0 pixels into connected regions (discarding regions ≤20px as noise). For each region it computes a centroid, an axis-aligned bbox, and an oriented bbox (`getRotatedBBox`). It builds a fully-connected graph of region centroids, sorts edges by distance, and runs Kruskal's algorithm (via union-find) to build a minimum spanning tree connecting all regions. It then draws a filled "capsule" (via `drawCapsule`) along each MST edge, sized to half the smaller oriented-bbox dimension of the two connected regions (or a global fallback `lineWidth`). Returns the resulting image (re-loaded from `canvas.toDataURL()`) with all disconnected ink blobs now visually bridged — used so multi-part artwork survives as a single connected stencil/cut shape.
- `checkImageMaskRegion(base64)` *(async)* — variant of `checkImageMask` using constants `MIN_REGION_SIZE=10`, `K_NEAREST=3`, `MIN_WIDTH_HEIGHT=2` to filter noise/thin regions. Instead of a fully-connected graph it builds a K-nearest-neighbor edge set (3 nearest neighbors per centroid) before running Kruskal's MST, and draws simple stroked lines (not capsules) between connected centroids, with `ctx.lineWidth` recomputed per edge as `max(1, 3/4 * min(bboxA, bboxB))`. Logs a warning and returns the original image if fewer than 2 regions are found. Returns the modified image.
- `connectObjectCenters(canvas)` — operates directly on a passed `<canvas>` (mutates it, no return value). Flood-fills alpha>0 regions (min size 50px), computes each region's centroid and a `size` (min of width/height), then draws a straight red line between **every pair** of regions (not just an MST) with stroke width `0.25 * min(sizeA, sizeB)`. Contains commented-out debug code for drawing region centers.
- `drawImageWidthAngle(img, canvas, angleDegrees, x, y, scaleX, scaleY, flipX=false, flipY=false)` — draws `img` onto `canvas`, translated to `(x,y)`, rotated by `angleDegrees`, and scaled by `ctx.scale(scaleX * fx, scaleY * fy)`. Note: `fx`/`fy` are referenced but never defined or imported anywhere in this file (not derived from the `flipX`/`flipY` parameters), so this method throws a `ReferenceError` if called as written — it appears to be unfinished/dead code.
- `getfiugerInfor(imageHole, width, height)` *(sic — "getFigureInfo")* — given a flat truthy/alpha mask array sized `width*height`, scans interior pixels (excluding the outer border row/column) and returns an array of `{x,y}` points for pixels that are set but have at least one 4-connected neighbor that is unset — i.e. the boundary pixels of the mask region.
- `getShapeInfo(imageHole, width, height)` — like `getfiugerInfor` but also explicitly tests the full top row, bottom row, and left/right edge columns, so it captures boundary pixels that touch the image edge as well as interior boundary pixels. Returns the full outline point list `{x,y}`.
- `findClosestPointByAngle(imageFurge, alpha, centerx, centery)` — given a set of boundary points and a target angle `alpha` (normalized into [-180,180]), finds the point in `imageFurge` minimizing a combined metric of angular difference times squared distance from `(centerx, centery)`. Returns the best match as `{x, y, distance, angle}` (fields on a sentinel object seeded with large values).
- `findHoleNearFuirge(imageHole, imageFurge, width, height, centerx, centery)` *(sic — "Fringe")* — locates the hole-mask pixel nearest `(centerx, centery)`, then uses `findClosestPointByAngle` at that angle ±90° against `imageHole` and against the boundary of `imageFurge` (via `getfiugerInfor`) to build a small 4-point quad `[point1, point3, point4, point2]` bridging a hole to the nearest outer edge. Returns that 4-point array.
- `detectAreaHoleAndFuirge(imageHole, imageFurge, width, height, centerx, centery)` — for each of 360 one-degree angle buckets relative to `(centerx, centery)`, finds the closest hole-mask pixel and closest furge(edge)-mask pixel in that bucket. Builds a final outline by preferring the furge point when available, falling back to the hole point. Returns an array of `{x,y}` points forming a polar-sampled combined boundary of the hole+edge masks.
- `checkAroundWhitePoint(data, location, width, max, valueCheck)` — boolean edge detector on a flat single-channel array: true if the value at `location` exceeds `valueCheck` and any of its 8 stride-offset neighbors (±4 for horizontal, ±`width` for vertical, and diagonals) is below `valueCheck`; bounds-checked against `max`. Used by `getPointSelect`.
- `calculatorPointWithAngle(center, point, angle)` — rotates `point` around `center` by `angle` degrees (via polar delta + angle offset) and returns the new coordinate as `{x, y, distance:1e8, x_near:0, y_near:0, angle:180, diff:1e8}` — a point pre-seeded with sentinel fields for later nearest-point search algorithms.
- `getPointCalculator(items, border)` — for each item with `mode_design.mode == "2"` (2D laser vector mode), reads `item.pathDLaserEx[0].data`, deduplicates consecutive identical points, rescales by `scaleX/scaleY` relative to the stored path scale, rotates each point about the item's center via `calculatorPointWithAngle` using `item.angle`, then offsets into border-relative cache coordinates using a `scaleOfCache` derived from `border.cacheWidth/width/zoomX/minScaleLimit`. Successive items' point lists are stitched together, appending to whichever end of the running polyline minimizes join distance (via `calculatorPoint`). Returns the concatenated point array (laser-cutting coordinate space).
- `getPointCalculatorSticker(items, border)` — same scaling/stitching logic as `getPointCalculator`, but reads `item.pathDLaser` and only processes items with `mode_stand == 1` (sticker "stand" pieces). Returns the concatenated point array.
- `getPointSelect(image_data, width, height, getBlackPoint=false, distance=5)` — two modes. Default (`getBlackPoint=false`): scans an RGBA buffer and returns a point for every pixel with alpha > 20, each pre-seeded with pathfinding sentinel fields (`distance, x_near, y_near, angle, diff`); the `distance` parameter is accepted but unused (its filtering logic is commented out, so all qualifying pixels are returned). `getBlackPoint=true`: scans interior pixels and uses `checkAroundWhitePoint` on the alpha channel (threshold 40) to return boundary pixels as `{x, y, distance:-1}`.
- `calculatorAngleAndPoint(p1, p2)` — computes the angular deviation from vertical (`|90 - atan2(dy,dx)|` in degrees) and squared distance between two points, plus a combined ranking score `angle*distance + distance`. Returns `{angle, distance, diff}`.
- `calculatorPoint(p1, p2)` — returns squared Euclidean distance plus a small linear penalty (`0.1*|dx| + 0.1*|dy|`), used as a distance/tie-break metric for point stitching (e.g. in `getPointCalculator`).
- `getNearPointInShape(imageHole, imageFurge)` — for every point in `imageHole`, finds the point in `imageFurge` minimizing `calculatorAngleAndPoint`'s `diff` score, and writes the match's `distance`, `angle`, `x_near`, `y_near` back onto the `imageHole` point (mutates in place). Returns the mutated `imageHole` array — a nearest-neighbor match between two point sets weighted by both angle and distance.
- `fillYellowPointCenter(newPoint, imageFurge)` — finds the extreme matched points A (max `x_near`) and B (min `x_near`) among `newPoint`; returns `[]` if `A.x === B.x`. Otherwise runs an internal grid-hash-accelerated BFS (`findMinPointPathFast`, with helper `dist2` and `buildGrid`) over `imageFurge` to find the shortest hop-connected path from A to B where consecutive points must be within radius `R=2`. Returns the ordered path of points (or `null` if unreachable).
- `checkAroundBlachWhite(data, location, width, max)` *(sic)* — boolean detector: true if the single-channel value at `location` equals 255 and at least one of its 8 stride-neighbors does not equal 255 (edge-of-solid-white-region detector).
- `addPixelArrayImage(data, width, height, numberPixel)` — mutates a flat RGBA pixel buffer `data` in place (no return value). First pass: binarizes it into solid red (alpha<10 → fully transparent, else opaque red `[255,0,0,255]`). Then builds a circular "stamp" mask (`arrayMaskInfo`, sized `(numberPixel+1)*2+1`) by sampling ring points at every 2° for radii 1..`numberPixel`. For every pixel flagged by `checkAroundBlachWhite` as being on a white/opaque boundary, it stamps the circular mask onto the buffer as semi-transparent yellow-green (`alpha=100`, green channel 255) wherever the target pixel isn't already fully opaque — effectively growing a colored halo/margin ring of radius `numberPixel` pixels around the artwork, likely to visualize a laser safety margin.
- `loadDialogueHTMLCarpet(sizeCarpet, FabricBorder, numberSide)` — pure string-template method (no DOM side effects) returning an HTML snippet (Japanese UI text) for a "carpet"/repeat-pattern preview modal, sized to `FabricBorder.width/height`, including a second `<canvas>` when `numberSide > 2`, and a close button.
- `drawImageCenter(ctx, img, x, y, w, h, angle)` — draws `img` scaled to `w×h`, centered at `(x,y)`, rotated by `angle` degrees around its own center (`save`/`translate`/`rotate`/`drawImage`/`restore`).
- `updateImageTo2DPreview(base64, preview_2d)` *(async)* — composites a design image (`base64`) onto a base preview image (`preview_2d.url`) at each placement described in `preview_2d.data` (array of `{x,y,w,h,a}` records, drawn via `drawImageCenter`). Returns `{data, width, height}` where `data` is a 2x-scaled PNG data URL and `width`/`height` are 2× the base image's dimensions; returns `{data:null, width:0, height:0}` if `preview_2d` is null/empty or on error (caught and logged to console).
- `showImageToHTMLCarpet(base64, chartCanvas, FabricBorder)` — loads an image and, on load, draws it into an existing DOM `<canvas>` (looked up by id `chartCanvas`), first resizing the canvas's CSS style and width/height attributes to `FabricBorder.width/height`. Side-effect only; no return value.
- `getloadPathBorder(side, get_hole=false)` — iterates `side.items._collection`, filtering to laser-mode items (`DrawTool.checkModeLaser`). For items carrying `imageCuttingLaser` plus a `pathDLaser`, it converts/clamps the path into cm-space relative to `side.border`/`FabricBorder` and pushes into `enableFuiger` (respecting `mode_stand`). For items with a plain `pathDLaser` (no `imageCuttingLaser`), it: computes cm-space translation offsets, converts each path point to polar coordinates around the item's own center, applies `flipX`/`flipY`, rotates by `item.angle`, and reconstructs Cartesian points (`newPointSvg`); if the item lacks `enableHole`, it computes the polygon's signed area/centroid and total turning angle to detect winding direction and reverses point order if needed for consistent winding. The points are then passed through `ImageCuttingLaser` (`initImageVectorPoint` / `getInfoDataVectorLaserBorder(2)`) to get a laser-safe simplified path, translated/clamped into the print border, and pushed into `enableHoleFuiger` (if `enableHole`) or `enableFuiger` (otherwise, respecting `mode_stand`). Returns `enableHoleFuiger` if `get_hole` is true, otherwise `enableFuiger` — arrays of `{data:[...flat x,y...], max_x, max_y, min_x, min_y, left, top}` objects representing per-item laser cut/hole border paths.
- `getInfoAcrylicLaser(side)` — for laser-mode items with `mode_design.mode == "1"` (acrylic/pedestal stand items) having `pathDLaserEx`, iterates all but (implicitly excludes handling of) the pedestal-base entry, computing cm-space translation via `DrawTool` helpers (`getBorderInfoLaser`, `getOptionBorderLaser`, `changePathDrawBorder`) plus a perpendicular center-offset correction based on item angle, then clamps each transformed point into the border bounds (`DrawTool.getLimitLeftPointLaser`/`getLimitTopPointLaser`), rounded to 2 decimals. Returns an array of `{data:[flat x,y pairs]}` — the acrylic piece's own laser cut path(s).
- `setBorderForPedestalOwner(itemSelectItem, ij, setupBorder)` — computes the translated/clamped border path for a single `pathDLaserEx[ij]` entry (same perpendicular-offset/scaling math as `getInfoAcrylicLaser`), iterating points in reverse order starting from the last point. Reads global context via `DrawTool.sides.selected` (rather than a `side` argument) for border/`FabricBorder` info. Returns a flat `[left, top, ...]` coordinate array — used to build the pedestal/stand base outline.
- `getHoleAcrylicLaser(side)` — for laser-mode items with `mode_design.mode == "1"` that have `pathDLaserEx`, processes only the **last** entry (the pedestal/base path) via `setBorderForPedestalOwner`, collecting `{data: pathBoder}` per item. Returns an array of pedestal "socket" hole border paths that the acrylic piece slots into.
- `getLocationHoleAcrylicLaser(side)` — finds the first laser-mode `mode_design.mode == "1"` item with more than one `pathDLaserEx` entry, and returns `{x, y, angle}` — the second entry's center point scaled by `side.border.cm.height/side.FabricBorder.height`, with the item's angle adjusted by that entry's `setup.angle_default` if present. Returns `null` if no matching item exists.
- `transformPoints(pointsData, angleDeg, scaleX, scaleY, centerx, centery)` — given a flat array of x,y pairs, computes their centroid, then for each point: recenters relative to the centroid, scales by `(scaleX, scaleY)`, rotates by `angleDeg`, and translates by `(centroid*scale + centerx/centery)`. Returns an array of `{x,y}` — a general affine transform (scale + rotate about the shape's own centroid, then place at a new origin), reused for laser hole-path placement.
- `findShortestLineToShapes(canvas, fromX, fromY)` — flood-fills a canvas's alpha>0 pixels into shapes (min size 20px), determines each shape's edge pixels (any 4-neighbor with alpha 0), and for each shape returns the single edge pixel closest (squared distance) to `(fromX, fromY)`. Returns an array of `{x,y}`, one nearest-boundary point per shape — used to find attachment/threading points relative to a reference location.
- `findCoverAreaHoleAndFuirge(arayHole, arayFurge, centerx, centery, listArray)` — for each target point in `listArray`, computes its angle from `(centerx, centery)`, then via `findClosestPointByAngle` locates two points on `arayHole` at that angle ±90° and two corresponding points on `arayFurge` at the same angle from those hole points, pushing a quad `{point:[point1,point3,point4,point2]}` per target. Returns an array of these quads — batched version of `findHoleNearFuirge` used to reinforce/patch multiple weak points at once.
- `isPointInPolygon(point, polygon)` — standard ray-casting point-in-polygon test over an array of `{x,y}` vertices; returns a boolean.
- `isPointInPolygonExactly(point, fiuger, width, height)` — bounds-checks `point` against `width`/`height`, then looks up membership in a flat mask array `fiuger` at index `floor(point.x + width*floor(point.y)) * 4`. Returns a boolean pixel-mask membership test.
- `distance(p1, p2)` — Euclidean distance via `Math.hypot(p1.x - p2.x, p1.y - p2.y)`.
- `minDistanceToVertices(point, shapePoints)` — returns the minimum `distance()` from `point` to any point in `shapePoints`.
- `checkHoleNearCutting(side)` *(async)* — validates that laser-cut "hole" geometry (e.g. chain-hanging holes) isn't placed too close to cut lines. Renders a transparent preview of `side` via `side.getImagePreviewTransparent`, then for each laser-mode item: if it has `enableHole`, transforms its hole path (`transformPoints`) and records it (with the item's on-canvas center/width) into `holeCover`; otherwise, it fills the item's cut-path polygon in yellow onto the internal scratch canvas. It derives a binary boundary mask (`imageFiurge`) and outline (`getShapeInfo`) from the composited canvas, and computes an allowed clearance `mmLaser` from `side.mmLaser`/`side.mmLaserEx` (scaled to pixels, zoom-adjusted, floored to 80%). For every hole point of every `holeCover` entry, it checks membership in the filled cut mask (`isPointInPolygonExactly`) and proximity to the outline (`minDistanceToVertices` vs `mmLaser`); if a violation is found it calls `showCanvasWithMarker` to display a warning and returns its result (truthy), otherwise returns `false`.
- `showCanvasWithMarker(base64, x, y, size=40, strokeWidth=3)` *(async)* — loads `base64` onto a new canvas, draws a `limegreen` stroked rectangle marker of `size` (clamped to stay within canvas bounds) centered at `(x,y)`, then displays the canvas inside a `Swal.fire` modal with a Japanese warning title ("holes for the chain and the cut line are too close together, please fix the design") and a "戻る" (back) confirm button. Returns `true` if the image loaded and the dialog was shown, `false` otherwise.
- `drawCapsule(ctx, x1, y1, x2, y2, width, color)` — draws a filled "capsule"/stadium shape of the given `width` connecting `(x1,y1)` to `(x2,y2)` (a rectangle with two semicircular end caps), filled with `color`. No-ops if the endpoints coincide. Used by `checkImageMask` to visually bridge disconnected regions.
- `privateAreaYellowing(canvas)` — mutates `canvas` in place via `ctx.putImageData`. Flood-fills from all four canvas edges to mark transparent pixels connected to the "outside" (`visited=1`), then flood-fills any remaining unvisited transparent pixels — enclosed interior transparent regions ("holes" inside a shape, e.g. the hole in a letter "O") — filling them fully opaque yellow (`255,255,0,255`, `visited=2`). No return value; used as a preprocessing step inside `checkImageMask` to distinguish enclosed holes from the surrounding transparent background before region/blob detection.

## src/utils/ImageTracer.js

**Purpose.** This is a raster-to-vector image tracer: given pixel data (from an `<img>`, `<canvas>`, or raw `ImageData`), it performs k-means style color quantization, traces the boundaries between color regions into pixel paths, smooths/simplifies those paths into line and quadratic Bézier segments, and emits the result either as an SVG `<path>` string or as an intermediate "tracedata" object (layers of paths + palette + dimensions). In UP-T it is used inside the 3D laser-engraving preview (`src/components/3d-laser-preview/index.jsx`) to convert a 2D canvas silhouette (a filled black shape drawn with `Path2D`/`ctx.fill`) into an SVG path string, which is then parsed with Three.js's `SVGLoader` to build extrudable `THREE.Shape`s for laser-cut acrylic stands, pedestals, holes, and figure cutouts. Only two of the library's many methods are actually called by the app: `imageToTracedataCanvas` (canvas → tracedata) and `getsvgstring` (tracedata → SVG string), both invoked with the `"grayscale"` option preset.

**Vendored: yes** — this is "imagetracer.js" version 1.2.6 by András Jankovics (andras@jankovics.net), released under The Unlicense (public domain), per the header/license block at the top of the file (lines 1–36). It is wrapped as a self-invoking singleton (`module.exports = new ImageTracer()` / `window.ImageTracer = new ImageTracer()`), matching the upstream project's distribution pattern. The internal vectorizing pipeline (color quantization → pathscan → internode interpolation → line/quadratic-spline fitting → SVG serialization) is standard, unmodified imagetracerjs internals and is not documented function-by-function here.

### Exports

The module exports a singleton `ImageTracer` instance with the following public API (all methods live on `this`, called as `ImageTracer.<method>(...)`):

- `imageToSVG(url, callback, options)` — Loads an image from a URL, traces it, and calls `callback(svgString)`.
- `imagedataToSVG(imgd, options)` — Traces an `ImageData` object directly and returns an SVG string.
- `imageToTracedata(url, callback, options)` — Loads an image from a URL and calls `callback(tracedata)`.
- `imageToTracedataCanvas(canvas, options)` — Traces the contents of a `<canvas>` element and returns a tracedata object synchronously. **(Used by UP-T.)** Called as `ImageTracer.imageToTracedataCanvas(canvas, "grayscale")` in `findInfoData()` of `3d-laser-preview/index.jsx`.
- `imagedataToTracedata(imgd, options)` — Core tracing routine: quantizes colors then produces `{ layers, palette, width, height }`, where each layer is an array of traced paths (segments with type `L`/line or `Q`/quadratic, each with coordinates).
- `getsvgstring(tracedata, options)` — Serializes a tracedata object into a full `<svg>...</svg>` string. **(Used by UP-T.)** Called as `ImageTracer.getsvgstring(tracedata, "grayscale")` immediately after `imageToTracedataCanvas` to get the final SVG path markup.
- `checkoptions(options)` — Normalizes an options argument: accepts a preset name (string, e.g. `"grayscale"`), a partial options object, or nothing, and fills in defaults from `optionpresets.default`.
- `optionpresets` — Object of named option bundles (`default`, `posterized1/2/3`, `curvy`, `sharp`, `detailed`, `smoothed`, `grayscale`, `fixedpalette`, `randomsampling1/2`, `artistic1-4`). UP-T's usage passes the string `"grayscale"`, which resolves to `{ colorsampling: 0, colorquantcycles: 1, numberofcolors: 7 }` merged over the defaults below.
- `appendSVGString(svgstr, parentid)` — Helper to inject an SVG string into a DOM container.
- `loadImage(url, callback, options)` / `getImgdata(canvas)` — Image-loading helpers used internally by the `imageTo*` methods.
- `drawLayers(layers, palette, scale, parentid)` — Debug helper that renders quantized color layers to canvases in a container div.
- A large set of lower-level pipeline functions are also attached to the instance (`colorquantization`, `samplepalette`, `samplepalette2`, `generatepalette`, `layering`, `layeringstep`, `pathscan`, `batchpathscan`, `internodes`, `batchinternodes`, `tracepath`, `fitseq`, `batchtracepaths`, `batchtracelayers`, `svgpathstring`, `blur`, `pointinpoly`, `testrightangle`, `getdirection`, `boundingboxincludes`, `torgbastr`, `tosvgcolorstr`, `compareNumbers`, `roundtodec`). These are internal building blocks of the vectorization algorithm and are not called directly by any code outside this file.

**Options object** (`optionpresets.default`, overridable per call and via named presets):

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| `corsenabled` | Sets `crossOrigin` on loaded images when tracing from a URL. |
| `ltres` | Line/error threshold for straight-line fitting during path simplification (lower = more points kept). |
| `qtres` | Error threshold for quadratic-spline fitting. |
| `pathomit` | Minimum path length (in pixels) to keep; shorter paths (noise) are discarded. |
| `rightangleenhance` | Detects and preserves sharp right-angle corners instead of smoothing them. |
| `colorsampling` | Palette generation method: `0` = generate an even palette, `1` = random pixel sampling, `2` = deterministic grid sampling. |
| `numberofcolors` | Target number of colors in the quantized palette. |
| `mincolorratio` | Minimum fraction of pixels a palette color must retain before being re-randomized during quantization cycles. |
| `colorquantcycles` | Number of k-means-style clustering refinement passes. |
| `layering` | `0` = sequential (trace one color layer at a time), non-zero = parallel/edge-based layering. |
| `strokewidth` | SVG stroke width for path outlines. |
| `linefilter` | Extra smoothing pass on line segments. |
| `scale` | Output scale multiplier applied to traced coordinates. |
| `roundcoords` | Decimal-place rounding applied to output coordinates (`-1` disables rounding). |
| `viewbox` | If true, emit an SVG `viewBox` attribute instead of fixed `width`/`height`. |
| `desc` | If true, include a `desc` attribute/element with tracer version info. |
| `lcpr`, `qcpr` | Stroke-width/circle radius used when rendering line/quadratic control points (debug visualization only). |
| `blurradius` | Gaussian-style selective blur radius applied before quantization (`0` disables). |
| `blurdelta` | Color-difference threshold controlling how the selective blur avoids blurring across strong edges. |
| `pal` | Optional explicit custom palette array (`[{r,g,b,a}, ...]`) overriding automatic palette generation. |
| `layercontainerid` | Optional DOM element id for rendering intermediate layer visualizations. |
